Theorem 216.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For convergence analysis pairs (A,B) in recognition theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 77 and the invariant I(A,B) = 109 mod 4.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the recognition decomposition. By minimality, the element a^77 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 109-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 216.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Under the structural decomposition assumption on recognition structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 348. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 305 with relations in degree 3.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 348 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the recognition structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 305. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 3. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
